   The broader goal is implementing the "Top-10 All Three Leaderboards: Phase 2 Sprint Plan" for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition ($1.1M prize). The previous session concluded MoE optimization at API ceiling (155.2µs vs 145µs leader). This session discovered that `AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1` actually changes fused_moe's default ksplit from 4→2 (not dead code as previously thought), and is now benchmarking two MoE variants to determine which is faster.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG env var behavior**: Sets `ksplit=2` (cktile path) instead of allowing fused_moe's default mixed dispatch (ksplit=4 for 256-expert shapes via cktile, ksplit=0 for 32-expert shapes via CK ASM)
   - **AITER_KSPLIT env var**: Confirmed dead code — fused_moe ignores the specific value (e.g., setting KSPLIT=0 still results in ksplit=2 when BYPASS is set)
   - **Two MoE kernel dispatch paths**: `module_moe_cktile2stages` (cktile, ksplit≥1) vs `module_moe_ck2stages_*` (CK ASM, ksplit=0). Each requires ~100s JIT build time
   - **Double JIT build timeout risk**: When both paths are triggered, total JIT time is ~257s (24.7s sorting + 106s cktile + 22s activation + 104s CK ASM), which can cause 12-minute GitHub Actions workflow timeout
   - **Per-expert-count dispatch**: fused_moe internally routes 256-expert shapes to cktile/ksplit=4 and 32-expert shapes to CK ASM/ksplit=0 when no BYPASS is set
   - **CK ASM path performance**: Dramatically slower for 32-expert shapes (44% worse for bs=16/32exp) compared to cktile path with ksplit=2
   - **Production adaptive KSPLIT (155.2µs geomean)**: Uses cktile for all shapes, avoiding CK ASM's poor 32-expert performance

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** (Current on disk - ksplit=0 forced version)
     - This is the main MoE submission file. Currently has BYPASS=1 which forces ksplit=2 via cktile path.
     - Being benchmarked as task b1a6pk6vg to measure ksplit=2 performance.
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 MoE: Force all shapes to ksplit=0 (CK ASM path).
     Hypothesis: CK ASM default is faster than cktile with ksplit=2.
     Also avoids double-JIT-build timeout.
     """
     import os
     import torch
     from task import input_t, output_t

     os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"
     os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
     os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "0"

     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
             gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
             gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, config,
         ) = data

         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]

         return fused_moe(
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
         )
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_opus_default.py`** (No BYPASS variant)

     ```python
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe

     os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"
     os.environ["AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING"] = "1"

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
             gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
             gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, config,
         ) = data

         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]

         return fused_moe(
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
         )
     ```

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1**: Forces all MoE shapes through cktile path (ksplit=2), avoiding CK ASM path which is 13-44% slower for 32-expert shapes. Production geomean: 155.2µs vs 168.7µs without BYPASS.
   - **AITER_KSPLIT env var**: Confirmed dead code — fused_moe ignores the specific value regardless of BYPASS setting.
   - **MoE JIT build budget**: sorting (25s) + cktile2stages (107s) + activation (23s) = ~155s. Combined with 7-shape benchmark (~500s) and GitHub Actions overhead (~120s), consistently exceeds the 12-minute workflow limit.
   - **GEMM tile configs**: Runner has three pre-compiled `.co` files: 32x128, 192x128, 256x256. The current submission uses 32x128 for all untuned shapes.
   - **Split-K overhead**: `log2_k_split=1` adds a reduce step after GEMM. For bandwidth-bound M=16/K=7168, the reduce overhead may exceed the parallelism benefit.
   - **Popcorn CLI transient failures**: Server was consistently slow all session (~3x normal), causing multiple timeouts. Artifact download failures and "not accepting submissions" errors also occurred.

3. Files and Code Sections:

     - **Edit 2 (kept)**: Removed split-K logic entirely. The `_get_config` function now reads:
     ```python
     def _get_config(M, N, K):
         """
         Improved configuration selection with adaptive tile sizing.
         Uses mathematical relationships to determine optimal tile sizes
         rather than relying solely on hardcoded thresholds.
         """
         key = (M, N, K)
         if key not in _config_cache:
             kernel_name = None
             split_k = None
             csv_found = False
             try:
                 cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
                 if isinstance(cfg, dict):
                     csv_found = True
                     kn = cfg.get("kernelName")
                     if kn is not None:
                         kernel_name = str(kn)
                     sk = cfg.get("splitK")
                     if sk is not None and int(sk) > 0:
                         split_k = int(sk)
                 elif cfg is not None:
                     csv_found = True
                     kernel_name = str(cfg)
             except Exception:
                 pass

             # Fallback: use known-valid tile configs from runner's .co files
             # Probe confirmed: 32x128, 192x128, 256x256 .co files on runner
             if kernel_name is None:
                 kernel_name = _kernel_name(32, 128)

             _config_cache[key] = (kernel_name, split_k)
         return _config_cache[key]
     ```
     - The docstring was updated to: `"""MXFP4 GEMM: Fused quant+shuffle + static buffer pre-allocation. 32x128 tile fallback, no split-K (reduce overhead removed)."""`
     - **What was removed**: The split-K block `if split_k is None and not csv_found and M <= 16 and K >= 4096: split_k = 1` was deleted. This removes the 2-way K-split for the M=16/K=7168 bottleneck shape.
     - Test passed 4/4 (0.0 max error) but benchmark timed out.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user's instruction (carried from previous context) was: "Continue the conversation from where it left off without asking the user any further questions." The broader goal is optimizing GPU kernels for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition ($1.1M prize) across three kernels (GEMM, MoE, MLA) on AMD MI355X. This session focused on GEMM optimization — specifically benchmarking the split-K-removed version from the prior session, and then discovering and fixing a critical HIP kernel bug that was wasting ~22s of the 720s workflow budget.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **HIP C++ kernel compilation via amdclang++**: Takes ~22s to compile for gfx950, eating into the 720s workflow timeout budget
   - **Silent compilation failure pattern**: A C++ syntax error (code at file scope) caused `subprocess.run(..., check=True)` to raise an exception, caught by `except Exception: return None`, making `_hip_lib = None` and silently falling through to Triton path
   - **Triton `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `e8m0_shuffle`**: The actual quantization path that was always running (the HIP "fused quant+shuffle" was never executing)
   - **Pre-allocated output buffers**: `_out_buf` dict caches `torch.empty(M, N, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")` to avoid per-call GPU allocation (~1-3µs savings)
   - **Popcorn CLI 12-minute workflow timeout**: JIT builds + benchmark execution must fit within 720s; HIP compilation overhead pushed submissions over the limit
   - **Competition shapes**: M=4/K=512, M=16/K=7168 (bottleneck), M=32/K=512 (x2), M=64/K=2048, M=256/K=1536

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (GEMM kernel)
     - This is the primary competition submission file for the MXFP4 GEMM kernel
     - **Major rewrite**: Removed all HIP C++ kernel code (~250 lines of dead code) and simplified to clean Triton-only path
     - Final file content (72 lines):
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 GEMM: Triton quant + ASM GEMM with pre-allocated output buffers.
     32x128 tile fallback, CSV config when available.
     """

     import torch
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter import dtypes
     import aiter
     from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
     from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
     from aiter.ops.gemm_op_a4w4 import get_GEMM_config

     _config_cache: dict = {}
     _out_buf: dict = {}

     def _kernel_name(tile_m, tile_n):
         sym = f"f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_{tile_m}x{tile_n}"
         return f"_ZN5aiter{len(sym)}{sym}E"

     def _get_config(M, N, K):
         key = (M, N, K)
         if key not in _config_cache:
             kernel_name = None
             split_k = None
             try:
                 cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
                 if isinstance(cfg, dict):
                     kn = cfg.get("kernelName")
                     if kn is not None:
                         kernel_name = str(kn)
                     sk = cfg.get("splitK")
                     if sk is not None and int(sk) > 0:
                         split_k = int(sk)
                 elif cfg is not None:
                     kernel_name = str(cfg)
             except Exception:
                 pass

             if kernel_name is None:
                 kernel_name = _kernel_name(32, 128)

             _config_cache[key] = (kernel_name, split_k)
         return _config_cache[key]

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         M, K = A.shape
         N = B.shape[0]

         kernel_name, log2_ks = _get_config(M, N, K)

         x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
         A_q_fp4x2 = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
         A_scale_sh = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)

         mn_key = (M, N)
         if mn_key not in _out_buf:
             _out_buf[mn_key] = torch.empty(M, N, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
         out = _out_buf[mn_key]

         return aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm(
             A_q_fp4x2,
             B_shuffle,
             A_scale_sh,
             B_scale_sh,
             out,
             kernel_name,
             bpreshuffle=True,
             log2_k_split=log2_ks,
         )
     ```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is optimizing GPU kernels for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition ($1.1M prize) across three kernels (GEMM, MoE, MLA) on AMD MI355X. This session had two phases:
   - **Phase 1**: Complete the in-flight GEMM test/benchmark submissions from the prior session (clean Triton-only submission with HIP dead code removed)
   - **Phase 2**: The user explicitly requested "custom kernel breakthroughs" — pushing beyond the API ceiling that all three kernels have hit. The goal is to close the performance gaps to competition leaders: GEMM (21.7µs vs 9.7µs, 2.2x), MoE (155µs vs 145µs, 1.07x), MLA (72µs vs 4.3µs, 17x).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MXFP4 quantization pipeline**: `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` (Triton) → `e8m0_shuffle` → `gemm_a4w4_asm` (ASM). The ~10-13µs quant overhead equals the GEMM time itself.
   - **torch._scaled_mm**: Native PyTorch 2.10 fused scaled matmul — discovered on the runner, could eliminate separate quantization step
   - **torch.compile with inductor backend**: Successfully fuses matmul+softmax+matmul into autotuned Triton BMM kernels for MLA decode
   - **fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort**: Newly discovered fused quant+sort kernel in aiter that could eliminate separate MoE sorting step
   - **35 pre-compiled ASM .co tiles**: Runner has tiles for 32x{128,256,384,512,640,768,1024}, 128x{128,256,384,512}, 160x{128,256,384}, 192x{128,256}, 224x{128,256}, 256x{128,256}
   - **Direct ck_moe_stage1/2 dispatch**: Bypass `fused_moe` wrapper by calling sorting + CK kernels directly
   - **12-minute server timeout**: All three kernels now timing out in leaderboard mode due to JIT build overhead + benchmark execution exceeding 720s
   - **Popcorn CLI submission workflow**: test → benchmark → leaderboard, with ~10min per submission round-trip

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (GEMM — current best, unchanged this session)

     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 GEMM: Triton quant + ASM GEMM with pre-allocated output buffers.
     32x128 tile fallback, CSV config when available.
     """
     import torch
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter import dtypes
     import aiter
     from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
     from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
     from aiter.ops.gemm_op_a4w4 import get_GEMM_config

     _config_cache: dict = {}
     _out_buf: dict = {}

     def _kernel_name(tile_m, tile_n):
         sym = f"f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_{tile_m}x{tile_n}"
         return f"_ZN5aiter{len(sym)}{sym}E"

     def _get_config(M, N, K):
         key = (M, N, K)
         if key not in _config_cache:
             kernel_name = None
             split_k = None
             try:
                 cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
                 if isinstance(cfg, dict):
                     kn = cfg.get("kernelName")
                     if kn is not None:
                         kernel_name = str(kn)
                     sk = cfg.get("splitK")
                     if sk is not None and int(sk) > 0:
                         split_k = int(sk)
                 elif cfg is not None:
                     kernel_name = str(cfg)
             except Exception:
                 pass
             if kernel_name is None:
                 kernel_name = _kernel_name(32, 128)
             _config_cache[key] = (kernel_name, split_k)
         return _config_cache[key]

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         M, K = A.shape
         N = B.shape[0]
         kernel_name, log2_ks = _get_config(M, N, K)
         x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
         A_q_fp4x2 = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
         A_scale_sh = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
         mn_key = (M, N)
         if mn_key not in _out_buf:
             _out_buf[mn_key] = torch.empty(M, N, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
         out = _out_buf[mn_key]
         return aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm(
             A_q_fp4x2, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
             out, kernel_name, bpreshuffle=True, log2_k_split=log2_ks,
         )
     ```

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`** (MLA — current best, unchanged)

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is optimizing GPU kernels for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition ($1.1M prize) on AMD MI355X across three kernels: GEMM (`amd-mxfp4-mm`), MoE (`amd-moe-mxfp4`), and MLA (`amd-mixed-mla`). This session continued from a context break, picking up where `submission_scaled_mm.py` had been created but not yet submitted. The goal was to discover and test new APIs that could close performance gaps: GEMM (21.7µs vs leader's 9.7µs, 2.2x), MoE (155µs vs 145µs, 1.07x), MLA (72µs vs 4.3µs, 17x).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MXFP4 quantization pipeline**: `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` (Triton) → `e8m0_shuffle` → `gemm_a4w4_asm` (ASM .co tiles)
   - **torch._scaled_mm**: Native PyTorch 2.10 fused scaled matmul — confirmed NOT working on MI355X (fp8fnuz not supported)
   - **gemm_a4w4_blockscale**: CK tile GEMM with block scales, signature `(XQ, WQ, x_scale, w_scale, Out, splitK=0)`, but rejects competition shapes ("This GEMM is not supported!")
   - **ck_moe_stage1/ck_moe_stage2**: The correct CK kernel names for MoE direct dispatch (NOT cktile_moe_gemm1/2 which don't exist). Require specific kernelName strings from CSV configs.
   - **fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort**: Fuses quantization with sorted token reordering. Signature: `(x, sorted_ids, num_valid_ids, token_num, topk, block_size=32, scaling_mode='even')`
   - **fused_flatten_mxfp4_quant**: Requires 3D input `(M, N1, N2)` — designed for MoE, not standalone GEMM
   - **fused_reduce_act_mul_and_mxfp4_quant**: Fuses SiLU + multiply + MXFP4 quant (inter-stage MoE kernel)
   - **torch.compile with inductor**: On MI355X, autotune selects hipBLAS vendor `bmm` kernel (identical to torch.matmul), no speedup
   - **fmoe_g1u1**: Opaque `torch.ops.aiter` JIT wrapper — calling convention unknown, dispatches via `getattr(torch.ops.aiter, loadName)(*args, **kwargs)`
   - **use_non_temporal_load vs non_temporal_load**: The Python JIT wrapper renames this parameter; the underlying torch.ops.aiter schema uses `use_non_temporal_load`
   - **CK kernel naming**: Kernel names encode dtype/scale/activation configuration (e.g., `module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_fp4x2_preshuffle_on_b16_silu_per_1x32_mulWeightStage2_`)
   - **12-minute server timeout**: JIT builds consume 100-200s, leaving limited time for benchmark execution
   - **All aiter GEMM functions discovered**: 50+ variants including batched_gemm_a8w8, deepgemm, gemm_a4w4_blockscale, etc.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is optimizing GPU kernels for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition ($1.1M prize) on AMD MI355X across three kernels: GEMM (`amd-mxfp4-mm`), MoE (`amd-moe-mxfp4`), and MLA (`amd-mixed-mla`). The explicit request from the prior context was "But we need custom kernel breakthroughs" — pushing beyond the Python API ceiling all three kernels have hit. This session focused specifically on discovering and testing the `fmoe_g1u1` single-dispatch ASM kernel as a potential MoE breakthrough, picking up from where `submission_fmoe_g1u1_probe.py` had been created but not submitted.

   Current leaderboard status (unchanged this session):
   - GEMM: ~14-23µs vs leader's 9.7µs (1.45-2.4x gap)
   - MoE: ~155µs vs leader's 145µs (1.07x gap)  
   - MLA: ~72-74µs vs leader's 4.3µs (17x gap)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **fmoe_g1u1**: A single-dispatch fused MoE ASM kernel that combines gate+up+down into ONE kernel launch (vs 2-stage ck_moe_stage1+stage2). Full schema: `fmoe_g1u1(out, input, gate, down, sorted_token_ids, sorted_weights, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, topk, input_scale, fc1_scale, fc2_scale, kernel_name, fc2_smooth_scale=None, activation=ActivationType.Silu)`
   - **MOEMetadata namedtuple**: What `get_2stage_cfgs` returns — contains `stage1`, `stage2` (functools.partial objects), `block_m`, `ksplit`, `run_1stage`, `has_bias`, `use_non_temporal_load`. NOT a kernel name string.
   - **get_2stage_cfgs signature**: `(token, model_dim, inter_dim, expert, topk, dtype, q_dtype_a, q_dtype_w, q_type, use_g1u1, activation, doweight_stage1, hidden_pad, intermediate_pad, is_shuffled=True)`
   - **fmoe_g1u1 input requirements**: Requires fp4x2 quantized activations (NOT bf16), e8m0-shuffled input scales as fp8_e8m0 dtype
   - **Pre-compiled ASM kernels**: Located at `/home/runner/aiter/hsa//gfx950/fmoe/silu/`, with names like `fmoe_bf16_pertokenMXfp4_g1u1_vs_silu_1tg_ps_32x512.co` and `fmoe_bf16_pertokenMXfp4_g1u1_vs_silu_2tg_ps_32x256.co`
   - **module_moe_asm JIT**: A separate JIT module from cktile, built via `hsa/codegen.py` (Pandas-based ISA codegen), takes ~31s to build
   - **dynamic_mxfp4_quant**: Returns `(x_fp4: uint8, x_scale_e8m0: uint8)`, must be viewed as `dtypes.fp4x2` and passed through `e8m0_shuffle` then viewed as `dtypes.fp8_e8m0`

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_g1u1_probe.py`** (Submitted to test, PASSED 3/3)
     - Probed torch.ops.aiter.fmoe_g1u1 schema, get_2stage_cfgs, fused_moe kernel selection
     - KEY DISCOVERY: Full fmoe_g1u1 schema and get_2stage_cfgs signature
     - Falls back to fused_moe for actual computation

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_g1u1_direct.py`** (Test PASSED 3/3 via fallback)
     - Attempted to use get_2stage_cfgs(use_g1u1=True) for kernel names
     - DISCOVERY: get_2stage_cfgs returns MOEMetadata namedtuple with kernelName='' (empty string) in the partials

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_g1u1_v2.py`** (Benchmark, fell back to fused_moe ~155µs)

     - ERROR: `fmoe_g1u1: input_scale needs to be <class 'torch.Tensor'> but got <class 'NoneType'>`
     - Confirmed competition benchmark shapes: all dhidden=7168, dexpert=256/512/2048, nroutedexperts=256/32

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_g1u1_v3.py`** (Test PASSED 3/3 via fallback)

     - Correctly passes fp4x2 quantized input + e8m0_shuffle'd scales
     - Shape 1 (bs=8, 256exp, dexpert=1024): **0.0 error** (bit-perfect!)
     - Shape 2 (bs=32, 32exp, dexpert=2048): **0.0 error** (bit-perfect!)
     - Shape 3 (bs=128, 64exp, dexpert=1536): **NaN** (521186 mismatches)
     - ASM kernel loaded: `fmoe_bf16_pertokenMXfp4_g1u1_vs_silu_1tg_ps_32x512`
     ```python
     x_fp4, x_scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(hidden_states.contiguous())
     x_q = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
     x_scale = e8m0_shuffle(x_scale_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
     _fmoe_g1u1(
         output, x_q, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
         sorted_token_ids, sorted_weights, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids,
         topk, x_scale, gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
         "", None, ActivationType.Silu,
     )
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_g1u1_v5.py`** (Test: 1/3 correct, 2/3 NaN — WORSE)

     - RESULT: Made things worse — shapes 2 AND 3 now produce NaN
     - CONFIRMED: e8m0_shuffle IS required on input scales

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_g1u1_hybrid.py`** (Benchmark: dexpert=256 OK, dexpert=512 ALL FAILED)
     - Hybrid approach: fmoe_g1u1 for d_expert % 512 == 0, fused_moe otherwise
     - dexpert=256 shapes: 139-313µs (worked, similar to fused_moe baseline)
     - dexpert=512 shapes: ALL FAILED correctness testing (unexpected — 512 % 512 == 0)
     - dexpert=2048 shapes: ALSO FAILED correctness
     - Two ASM kernels loaded: `32x256` and `32x512`
     - The d_expert % 512 alignment heuristic is INSUFFICIENT
     ```python
     can_use_g1u1 = (
         _direct_ok
         and shape_key not in _failed_shapes
         and d_expert % 512 == 0
     )
     ```

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **`get_2stage_cfgs` returns MOEMetadata, not kernel names**: Passed entire object as kernelName string. Not fixed — discovered MOEMetadata contains `kernelName=''` in the partials, meaning BYPASS=1 uses empty string.
   - **`input_scale needs to be Tensor not NoneType`**: fmoe_g1u1 requires pre-quantized activations unlike fused_moe. Fixed by adding `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` in v3.
   - **`unsupport current input type:BFloat16`**: fmoe_g1u1 expects fp4x2 input, not bf16. Fixed in v4 by passing `x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)`.
   - **NaN on dexpert=1536 (v4)**: Shape 3 produces NaN. Hypothesis was dimension alignment. NOT FIXED — per-shape fallback added in hybrid.
   - **Removing e8m0_shuffle made MORE shapes fail (v5)**: Confirmed e8m0_shuffle IS required on input scales. Reverted to shuffled approach.
   - **dexpert=512 shapes fail in benchmark despite 512%512==0 (hybrid)**: The alignment heuristic is wrong. The test shapes (dexpert=1024, 2048) that passed are different from benchmark shapes (dexpert=256, 512, 2048). The benchmark showed dexpert=512 shapes fail, which contradicts the alignment theory. NOT FIXED — investigation needed.

5. Problem Solving:
   **Solved:**
   - Full fmoe_g1u1 calling convention discovered (schema, input types, scale format)
   - Confirmed fmoe_g1u1 can produce bit-perfect results (0.0 error) for certain shapes
   - Confirmed e8m0_shuffle IS required for input activation scales
   - get_2stage_cfgs return type documented (MOEMetadata namedtuple)
   
   **Ongoing:**
   - fmoe_g1u1 produces NaN/wrong results on many shapes. The pattern is unclear:
     - TEST mode: Works for dexpert=1024, 2048. Fails for dexpert=1536.
     - BENCHMARK mode: Works for dexpert=256. Fails for dexpert=512, 2048.
     - This inconsistency suggests the issue isn't just dimension alignment — it could be related to num_experts, topk, batch size, or the specific ASM kernel tile config selected.
   - Even where fmoe_g1u1 works (dexpert=256 benchmark), timings are ~139-313µs — NOT faster than fused_moe baseline (~141-355µs). The single-dispatch advantage is offset by the added `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `e8m0_shuffle` overhead that fused_moe handles internally.

8. Current Work:
   Immediately before this summary, I had just received benchmark results for `submission_fmoe_g1u1_hybrid.py`. The results showed:
   - dexpert=256 shapes (256 experts): Worked correctly, timing 139-313µs
   - dexpert=512 shapes (32 experts): ALL FAILED correctness testing — unexpected since 512 % 512 == 0
   - dexpert=2048 shapes (32 experts): ALSO FAILED correctness
   - Benchmark status: "❌ Benchmarking failed" due to correctness failures on 4/7 shapes

   The hybrid approach needs a more sophisticated shape filter than `d_expert % 512 == 0`. Looking at the data:
   - TEST pass: dexpert=1024 (256exp), dexpert=2048 (32exp) — both with topk=8/9
   - TEST fail: dexpert=1536 (64exp) — topk=6
   - BENCHMARK pass: dexpert=256 (256exp) — topk=8
   - BENCHMARK fail: dexpert=512 (32exp), dexpert=2048 (32exp) — topk=8

   The common factor in passing shapes is nroutedexperts=256. All 32-expert shapes fail. This could be related to the ASM kernel's expert routing assumptions.

9. Optional Next Step:
   The immediate next step would be to refine the fmoe_g1u1 hybrid filter to only use fmoe_g1u1 for shapes with nroutedexperts=256 (which all passed both test and benchmark). However, early benchmark data shows fmoe_g1u1 is NOT faster than fused_moe (~139µs vs ~141µs for the first benchmark shape — within noise). The `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `e8m0_shuffle` overhead (~10-15µs) negates the single-dispatch advantage.

   Before investing more submissions, it's worth assessing whether fmoe_g1u1 can ever be faster than fused_moe for these shapes. If the quant overhead is unavoidable, fmoe_g1u1 is a dead end for performance improvement despite being a novel API path. The comparison so far:
   - fused_moe baseline (dexpert=256, bs=16): ~141µs
   - fmoe_g1u1 hybrid (dexpert=256, bs=16): ~139µs (within noise)

   Alternatively, use `fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort` (discovered in prior session) to fuse the quant+sort step before calling fmoe_g1u1, which could eliminate the separate quant overhead.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale`**: CK tile GEMM with block-level scales. Signature: `(XQ, WQ, x_scale, w_scale, Out, splitK=0)`. JIT build: 195s. Only supports shapes in `a4w4_blockscale_tuned_gemm.csv` (1471 entries). Fails with "This GEMM is not supported!" for untuned shapes. Only 2/6 competition shapes have tuned configs, and they use the SAME kernel tile as the ASM default — confirmed dead end.
   - **`moe_cktile2stages_gemm1/gemm2`**: CK tile MoE functions that do NOT require `kernelName` parameter (unlike `ck_moe_stage1/2`). But trigger their own JIT module build that causes 12-minute timeout.
   - **`ck_moe_stage1/2` `kernelName` requirement**: Cannot pass `None` — strict type check `TypeError: kernelName needs to be <class 'str'>`. Requires pre-selected kernel name from tuned CSV configs.
   - **`expert_mask` in `fused_moe`**: Triggers a DIFFERENT CK module build (`no_mulWeightStage2` suffix) that takes ~106s additional JIT, causing 12-minute timeout on fresh runners.
   - **`get_2stage_cfgs`**: Kernel name selection function for MoE CK stages. Signature: `(token, model_dim, inter_dim, expert, topk, dtype, q_dtype_a, q_dtype_w, q_type, use_g1u1, activation, doweight_stage1, hidden_pad, intermediate_pad, is_shuffled=True)`.
   - **`fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort`**: Fuses quantization with token sorting. Signature: `(x, sorted_ids, num_valid_ids, token_num, topk, block_size=32, scaling_mode='even')`.
   - **`moe_sorting_fwd`**: Accepts `local_expert_mask` and `dispatch_policy` parameters — proper way to pass expert masking at sort level.
   - **12-minute workflow timeout**: Fundamental constraint — JIT builds consume 130-230s, leaving only ~8-9 minutes for actual benchmarking. Any non-standard CK dispatch path triggers additional JIT modules.
   - **aiter version on runner**: Torch 2.10.0+rocm7.1, aiter git HEAD f3be04a12.

     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 MoE: BYPASS=1 cktile path, proven ~155us baseline.
     Restored from Phase 17 — expert_mask triggers extra JIT build that
     causes 12-minute timeout on fresh runners.
     """
     import os
     import torch
     from task import input_t, output_t
     os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"
     os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
     os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "0"
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
          gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
          gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
          gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
          topk_weights, topk_ids, config) = data
         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
         return fused_moe(
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
         )
     ```

   - **`gemm_afp4wfp4` `_get_config` Triton configs**: Returns per-shape tile configs with `NUM_KSPLIT`. Small-M shapes (M=4,16) get NUM_KSPLIT=16; larger shapes get NUM_KSPLIT=1. Config includes BLOCK_SIZE_M/N/K, GROUP_SIZE_M, num_warps, waves_per_eu, matrix_instr_nonkdim, cache_modifier.
   - **`gemm_a4w4_asm` `log2_k_split`**: ASM GEMM accepts `log2_k_split` parameter. `log2_k_split=1` splits K into 2 pieces, `=2` splits into 4. Confirmed correct with 0.0 error for K=7168.
   - **192x128 ASM tile**: Second pre-compiled `.co` file at `/home/runner/aiter/hsa/gfx950/f4gemm/`. Works correctly for M>=128 shapes.
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale`**: CK tile GEMM with block-level scales. JIT build takes 203s. Produces WRONG results when combined with `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `e8m0_shuffle` quantization path (16724 mismatches for shape 8,2112,7168). Dead end.
   - **`moe_sorting_fwd` full signature**: `(topk_ids, topk_weights, sorted_token_ids, sorted_weights, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, moe_buf, num_experts, unit_size, local_expert_mask=None, num_local_tokens=None, dispatch_policy=0)`

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is optimizing GPU kernels for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition ($1.1M prize) on AMD MI355X (gfx950, ROCm 7.1, Torch 2.10.0+rocm7.1) across three kernels: GEMM (`amd-mxfp4-mm`), MoE (`amd-moe-mxfp4`), and MLA (`amd-mixed-mla`). The original request from prior sessions was "we need custom kernel breakthroughs." This session continued GEMM optimization from a context compaction with the instruction to "Continue the conversation from where it left off without asking the user any further questions." The focus was on discovering alternative GEMM API paths that could beat the current `dynamic_mxfp4_quant + gemm_a4w4_asm` pipeline (~21µs geomean vs leader's 9.7µs, 2.2x gap).

   Current leaderboard status:
   - GEMM: ~21µs geomean vs leader's 9.7µs (2.2x gap)
   - MoE: ~155µs vs leader's 145µs (1.07x gap)
   - MLA: ~72µs vs leader's 4.3µs (16.7x gap)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **`gemm_a4w4_asm`** — Hand-coded ASM kernel using `.co` binaries loaded via `hipModuleLoad`. Our current production GEMM path. Only 2 pre-compiled kernels: 32x128 and 192x128 tiles.
   - **`gemm_a4w4`** (non-ASM) — JIT-compiled Triton persistent kernel path. Same function the reference uses. 5-14% slower than ASM for small M.
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale`** — CK (Composable Kernel) tile implementation. Schema: `(XQ, WQ, x_scale, w_scale, Out, splitK=0)`. JIT build ~218s. FAILS with "This GEMM is not supported!" for actual test shapes.
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale_tune`** — Same as blockscale with explicit `kernelId` selector: `(XQ, WQ, x_scale, w_scale, Out, kernelId, splitK=0)`.
   - **`_scaled_mm_v2`** — PyTorch aten op that exists but uses `List[Tensor]` for scale_a (grouped MM schema). Dead end for standalone GEMM.
   - **`_quant_mxfp4`** — Reference's quant function, revealed to be a thin wrapper: `dynamic_mxfp4_quant(x)` + `e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0)` + `.view()`. No performance advantage.
   - **`dynamic_mxfp4_quant`** — The patched Triton quantization kernel (PR #975). Takes ~23.6µs for M=8,K=7168 — the DOMINANT bottleneck.
   - **`LLMM1(in_a, in_b, out_c, rows_per_block)`** — bf16 block GEMM, not applicable for fp4.
   - **`hipb_mm(in_a, in_b, solution_index: int)`** — hipBLAS MM with solution selector, bf16 only.
   - **`rocb_mm(in_a, in_b, arg2: int)`** — rocBLAS MM with solution index, bf16 only.
   - **Quant overhead dominates**: For K=7168, quant=23.6µs vs GEMM=~7-10µs. Total pipeline ~30µs. Leader at 9.7µs must fuse quant into GEMM.

     ```python
     def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         """Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given."""
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         A = A.contiguous()
         B = B.contiguous()
         m, k = A.shape
         n, _ = B.shape
         # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path
         A_q, A_scale_sh = _quant_mxfp4(A, shuffle=True)
         out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
             A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
             dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
         )
         return out_gemm
     ```

   - **_quant_mxfp4 source (discovered this session via probe):**
     ```python
     def _quant_mxfp4(x, shuffle=True):
         x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(x)
         if shuffle:
             bs_e8m0 = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0)
         return x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2), bs_e8m0.view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
     ```

   - **generate_input source (discovered this session via probe):**
     ```python
     def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):
         assert k % 64 == 0
         gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
         gen.manual_seed(seed)
         A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
         B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
         B_q, B_scale_sh = _quant_mxfp4(B, shuffle=True)
         B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
         return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
     ```

4. Errors and fixes:

   - **`_scaled_mm_v2` fp4 rejection**: `Expected a value of type 'List[Tensor]' for argument 'scale_a' but instead found type 'Tensor'`. This is a grouped matmul op requiring List[Tensor] scales — fundamentally incompatible with standalone GEMM. No fix; dead end confirmed.
   
   - **`gemm_a4w4` (non-ASM) regression**: 5-14% slower than ASM for M<=32. Root cause: Python wrapper overhead in the JIT dispatch chain. No fix needed; confirmed ASM path is superior.
   
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale` "This GEMM is not supported!"**: The CK tile kernel accepted inputs in probe context (where it was called once alongside the ASM fallback) but CRASHED when used as the primary path. The error comes from `aiter/jit/core.py:971` — the CK kernel internally checks shape support and rejects unsupported M/N/K combinations. The probe succeeded because it was called with the first test shape's data (M=8, N=2112, K=7168) which happened to be a multiprocessing fork where the eval harness called it differently. Actually, the probe succeeded in the probe file where both blockscale and ASM were loaded — but when blockscale is the ONLY path, the eval harness calls it in a subprocess (multiprocessing.pool) with a cloned data, and the shape support check fails. This is either a shape mismatch or the CK kernel only supports a subset of shapes.
   
   - **Write tool error**: Attempted to write `submission_blockscale.py` without reading it first. Fixed by reading the existing file first, then using Write to overwrite.

5. Problem Solving:

   **Solved/Confirmed this session (Phase 17 findings):**
   
   | Path | Result | Root Cause |
   |------|--------|------------|
   | `_scaled_mm_v2` with fp4 | `List[Tensor]` required for scales | Grouped MM schema, not standalone GEMM |
   | `_scaled_grouped_mm` | Exists but not applicable | Grouped operation |
   | `gemm_a4w4` (non-ASM, Triton persistent) | 5-14% slower than ASM | Python wrapper overhead |
   | `_quant_mxfp4` vs `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` | Identical underlying function | `_quant_mxfp4` = thin wrapper adding e8m0_shuffle |
   | `gemm_a4w4_blockscale` (CK tile) | "This GEMM is not supported!" | CK kernel doesn't support all shape/config combinations |
   | `LLMM1`, `hipb_mm`, `rocb_mm` | bf16 only, not fp4 | Different problem domain |
   | `gemm_a4w4_blockscale_tune` | Needs `kernelId` parameter | Could work if correct kernelId is found |

   **Complete dead ends confirmed this session (do not retry):**
   - `_scaled_mm_v2` — grouped MM, wants List[Tensor] for scales
   - `gemm_a4w4` (non-ASM) — slower than ASM for all shapes
   - `gemm_a4w4_blockscale` — "This GEMM is not supported!" on test shapes
   - `LLMM1`, `hipb_mm`, `rocb_mm` — bf16 only, not for MXFP4

   **Remaining unexplored paths:**
   - `gemm_a4w4_blockscale_tune` with specific `kernelId` values — could target supported shapes
   - `gemm_a4w4_blockscale` with `splitK>0` — might enable support for more shapes
   - The fundamental bottleneck remains: quant overhead (~23µs for K=7168) cannot be eliminated via any Python API

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale`** — CK (Composable Kernel) tile implementation. Schema: `(XQ, WQ, x_scale, w_scale, Out, splitK=0)`. JIT build ~213-219s. Supports some shapes but not all. splitK=0,1,2 work; splitK>=4 crashes with "This GEMM is not supported!".
   - **`gemm_a4w4_blockscale_tune`** — Same with explicit `kernelId` selector: `(XQ, WQ, x_scale, w_scale, Out, kernelId, splitK=0)`. JIT build starts AFTER blockscale build; combined timeout exhaustion prevented testing.
   - **`gemm_a4w4_asm`** — Hand-coded ASM kernel using pre-compiled `.co` binaries. Remains the fastest GEMM path.
   - **MLA hybrid routing** — Three regimes: matmul (small shapes), direct ASM a16w8 (medium), direct ASM a8w8 (large). OR routing: `bs <= 4 OR total_kv <= 32768`.
   - **Adaptive `num_kv_splits`** — Schedule from Phase 13: 1→4→8→16→32 based on total_kv thresholds.
   - **Direct ASM dispatch** — Calling `mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd` and `mla_reduce_v1` directly bypasses `mla_decode_fwd` wrapper overhead (~2-5µs savings).
   - **JIT build times** — blockscale: ~213-219s, ASM GEMM: ~20s, MLA metadata: ~32s, MLA ASM: ~21s, MLA reduce: ~171s.

     ```python
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         M, K = A.shape
         N = B.shape[0]
         x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
         A_q = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
         A_scale = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
         mn_key = (M, N)
         if mn_key not in _out_buf:
             _out_buf[mn_key] = torch.empty(M, N, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
         out = _out_buf[mn_key]
         shape_key = (M, N, K)
         route = _route_cache.get(shape_key)
         if route != "asm":
             try:
                 result = aiter.gemm_a4w4_blockscale(
                     A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale, B_scale_sh, out, 0,
                 )
                 _route_cache[shape_key] = "blockscale"
                 return result
             except RuntimeError:
                 _route_cache[shape_key] = "asm"
         # ASM fallback
         kernel_name = None
         try:
             cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
             if isinstance(cfg, dict):
                 kn = cfg.get("kernelName")
                 if kn is not None:
                     kernel_name = str(kn)
         except Exception:
             pass
         if kernel_name is None:
             kernel_name = _kernel_name_default()
         return aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm(
             A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale, B_scale_sh,
             out, kernel_name, bpreshuffle=True,
         )
     ```

     ```python
     MATMUL_MAX_BS = 4
     MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 32768
     A16W8_THRESHOLD = 262144

     def _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:
         if total_kv <= 2048:    return 1
         if total_kv <= 16384:   return 4
         if total_kv <= 131072:  return 8
         if total_kv <= 524288:  return 16
         return 32

     def _matmul_path(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads):
         kv_bf16 = kv_data["bf16"]
         kv = kv_bf16.view(bs, kvseqlen, QK_HEAD_DIM)
         q_3d = q.view(bs, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM)
         kv_t = kv.transpose(1, 2)
         scores = torch.matmul(q_3d, kv_t).mul_(SM_SCALE)
         weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
         v = kv[:, :, :V_HEAD_DIM]
         out = torch.matmul(weights, v)
         return out.unsqueeze(1).reshape(-1, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM)

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         # ... unpack data ...
         total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
         use_matmul = (bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS) or (total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV)
         if use_matmul:
             return _matmul_path(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads)
         # Direct ASM with adaptive splits...
         num_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)
         # ... metadata caching, buffer pre-allocation, stage1+reduce calls ...
     ```
